:
Extension-Factorization Lemma

Lemma 5.4

(Extension-Factorization)
.

In the setting of Lemma
5.3
, let
\omega
be a Hölder-like modulus, and either suppose that:

(i)

\varphi:(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})\hookrightarrow(F_{\varphi},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}})
be a quasisymmetric map into a separable Banach space
F_{\varphi}
and for every compact subset
K\subseteq\mathcal{X}
the restriction of
\varphi^{-1}
to
\varphi(K)
has a Hölder-like modulus of continuity
\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}
,

(ii)

\varphi(K)
is a doubling subset of
F_{\varphi}
(esp. if
K
is finite) and the restriction of
\varphi^{-1}
to
\varphi(K)
has a Hölder-like modulus of continuity
\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}
.

Then, for every compact doubling set
K
and every
f:K\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
with Hölder-like modulus of continuity
\omega
, there exists a constant
C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}>0
(depending only on
K
,
\omega
,
\varphi^{-1}
, and on
F_{\varphi}
) and a map
f_{\varphi}:F_{\varphi}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
satisfying

(1)

Factorization:

f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(x)=f(x)
for all
x\in K
,

(2)

Stability:

f_{\varphi}
has the following Hölder-like modulus of continuity:

C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}.

Proof of Lemma
5.4
.

Since
\varphi
is injective,
\varphi^{-1}
exists on
\varphi(\mathcal{X})
. Because
K
is compact and
\varphi
is continuous,
\varphi(K)
is itself compact; as a consequence,
\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}
is uniformly continuous on
K
. We denote its modulus of continuity thereon by
\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}
. Then the map
f\circ\varphi^{-1}:\varphi(K)\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is well-defined and uniformly continuous, with modulus of continuity
\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}.

If Condition (i) holds
: Since
\varphi
is a quasisymmetric map and since
K
is doubling then,
[
67
, Theorem 10.18]
implies that
\varphi(K)
is doubling. Thus the conditions of Lemma
5.3
are met and there is a uniformly continuous extension,
\mathcal{E}(f\circ\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}):F_{\varphi}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, of
f
from
\varphi(K)
to all of
F_{\varphi}
with modulus of continuity

\omega_{\mathcal{E}(f\circ\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1})}=c\log(C_{(K,\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}\circ\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}})})\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}(t).

(5.3)

We set
f_{\varphi}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathcal{E}(f\circ\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1})
and set
C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}c\log\big(C_{(K,\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}\circ\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}})}\big)
.

If Condition (ii) holds
: Then
\varphi(K)
is doubling. We argue precisely as in case (i).
In either case, by construction. we have that for every
x\in K
since
\varphi(x)\in\varphi(K)
then

f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(x)=f\circ\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}\circ\varphi(x)=f(x).

∎

The next lemma is illustrated in Figure
14
(where the dashed line represents
\varepsilon
-approximate commutation). The result is an approximate version of Lemma
5.4
where the maps
\varphi
and
f_{\varphi}
are approximated by passing through the finite-dimensional subspace of
F_{\varphi}
defined in (
2.10
).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,d_{\mathcal{X}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{\varphi},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{T_{n}}^{-1}\circ T_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\varphi:n}}

Figure 14
:
Approximate Extension-Factorization Lemma

Lemma 5.5

(Approximate Extension-Factorization)
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathcal{X}
be non-empty and compact,
\varphi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow F_{\varphi}
be a feature map,
(T_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence of bounded linear operators on
F_{\varphi}
implementing the
C_{BAP:T_{\cdot}}
-BAP on
\varphi(K)
, and
assume that any of conditions (i)-(ii) in Lemma
5.4
holds.
For every
\omega
-Hölder-like map
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
and each “encoding error”
\varepsilon_{E}>0
, there is an
n_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
, a map
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}:(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow({\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n_{\varepsilon_{E}}})
, and a uniformly continuous map

f_{\varphi:n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}:({\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n_{\varepsilon_{E}}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})

:

(i)

Approximation:

\sup_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\left(f(x),f_{\varphi:n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x)\right)\leq\varepsilon_{E},

(ii)

Regularity:
The map
\omega_{f_{\varphi:n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}}(t)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}C_{(\omega,K,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}\,\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}\big(C_{\operatorname{BAP}:T_{\cdot}}t\big)
is a modulus of continuity for
f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}
,

(iii)

Approximate Feature-Map Representation:

\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\iota_{T_{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}}^{-1}\circ T_{{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}}\circ\varphi.

Moreover, the integer
n_{\varepsilon_{E}}
is given by

n_{\varepsilon_{E}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\min\Big\{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:\,\max_{u\in\varphi(K)}\,\big\|u-T_{n}(u)\big\|_{F_{\varphi}}\leq\Big(\omega^{\dagger}_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}\circ\omega^{\dagger}\Big(\frac{\varepsilon_{E}}{C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}}\Big)\Big)\Big\}

where
C_{(K,f,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi},T_{\cdot})}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}C_{(K,f,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}>0
and
C_{(K,f,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}>0
is defined in Lemma
5.4
, it depends only on
K,f,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi}
, and on
(T_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
and on which of the conditions Lemma
5.4
(i) or (ii) hold.
Furthermore,
N_{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
denotes the rank of
T_{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

By Lemma
5.4
, there is a map
f_{\varphi}:(F_{\varphi},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
satisfying
f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(x)=f(x)
for every
x\in K
. For every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
define the map
f_{\varphi:n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}f_{\varphi}\circ\iota_{T_{n}}:({\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
and define the map
\varphi^{(n)}:\iota_{T_{n}}^{-1}\circ T_{n}\circ\varphi:(K,d_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow({\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})
.

Since the operators
(T_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
realize the
C_{BAP:T_{\cdot}}
-BAP on
\varphi(K)
and since
\varphi
is injective, we have that for every
x\in K
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),f_{\varphi:n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x)\Big)

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(x),f_{\varphi:n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x)\Big)

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(x),\big(f_{\varphi}\circ\iota_{T_{n}}\big)\circ\big(\iota_{T_{n}}^{-1}\circ T_{n}\circ\varphi(x)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(x),f_{\varphi}\circ T_{n}\circ\varphi(x)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{f_{\varphi}}\left(\big\|\varphi(x)-T_{n}\circ\varphi(x)\big\|_{F_{\varphi}}\right)

\displaystyle=C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}\left(\big\|\varphi(x)-T_{n}\circ\varphi(x)\big\|_{F_{\varphi}}\right).

By the monotonicity of the modulus of continuity
\omega_{f_{\varphi}}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),f_{\varphi:n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x)\Big)\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\varphi(K)}\,C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}\big(\|u-T_{n}(u)\big\|_{F_{\varphi}}\big).

Setting
n_{\varepsilon_{E}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}R^{T_{\cdot}:\varphi(K)}\Big(\omega_{f_{\varphi}}^{-1}\big(\varepsilon_{E}\big)\Big)
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\varphi^{(n_{\varepsilon_{E}})}
, where
\omega_{f_{\varphi}}=C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}
, and noting that
\operatorname{Lip}(T_{n_{\varepsilon}})=\|T_{n_{\varepsilon}}\|_{op}\leq C_{BAP:F_{\varphi}}
, yields the conclusion.
∎

To obtain the “local version” of our main result, it only remains to approximate the maps
f_{\varphi:\varepsilon}
constructed in Lemma
5.5
. However, since all norms on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are equivalent, under the assumption that
(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
is a QAS space, we only need a mild variant of the main “static result” of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
. We now present this variant, adapted to our context. We note that if
A
is a subset of a Euclidean space, then
A
is doubling; see
[
67
, Section 10.13]
or
[
67
, Theorem 12.1]
for details).

Lemma 5.6

(Approximation of Functions on Finite-Dimensional Banach Space if
\mathcal{Y}
is a Barycentric QAS Space)
.

Let
K
be a non-empty compact subset of the finite-dimensional Banach space
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})
and let
C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})}\geq 0
be
K
’s doubling constant,
(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
admit a quantized mixing function
\hat{\eta}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\cdot}
be a universal approximator, and
f:(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
be an
\omega
-Hölder-like function. For each “quantization error”
\varepsilon_{Q}>0
and each “approximation error”
\varepsilon_{A}>0
, there exists:
c,N,q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, an
\hat{f}_{n,N}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,N,c}
, and
Z=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times D_{q}}
such that

\sup_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n,N}(x),\big(Z_{n}\big)_{n=1}^{N}\big)\Big)\leq\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q}.

Furthermore,
c,N,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
are bounded above by

(i)

c\leq\left\lceil r^{\dagger}(C_{\omega,F_{\varphi},n}\omega,K,n,N,\cdot)\left(C_{\eta,\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}\,\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right\rceil
,

(ii)

\ln(N)\leq C_{\omega}\ln\big(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})}\big)\Big\lceil-\log_{2}\big(C_{\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}\omega^{\dagger}\big(C^{\prime}_{\eta,\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}\,\varepsilon_{A}\big)\big)\Big\rceil

(iii)

D\leq\mathscr{Q}_{f(\mathcal{X})}(\varepsilon_{Q})

.

The constants
C_{\eta,\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n},\,C_{\omega},\,C_{\omega,F_{\varphi},n},\,C_{\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n},\,C^{\prime}_{\eta,\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}>0
each depends only on the quantities appearing in their respective indices.

Comment on the proof of Lemma
5.6
.

The steps in proving this lemma are essentially identical to those of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
’s proof up to two minor modifications, which we now discuss.

First Modification of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
’s proof - Steps
1
and
2

Since we do not require that
f
is
\alpha
–Hölder continuous
(which is the case in
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
)
but rather that it is uniformly continuous with a Hölder-like modulus of continuity
\omega
, Lemma
5.2
implies that the upper-bound in
[
44
, Equation (6.21)]
(on the Lipschitz constant of the random projection of
(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}^{\omega})
to any closed subset in
K
; in the sense of
[
78
]
) is

2c\log\big(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}^{\omega})}\big)\leq 2c\lceil-\log_{2}(h_{\omega}^{\dagger}(1/4))/4\rceil\log(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})})\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}C_{\Pi},

for some absolute constant
c>0
. Accordingly, we modify the constant
\delta>0
defined in
[
44
, Equation (6.23)]
to be

\delta\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\omega^{\dagger}\Big(\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{3C_{\eta}C_{\Pi}}\Big).

Together, Lemmata
5.2
and
[
44
, Lemma 6.1]
therefore
imply that the estimate on the external covering number of
f(K)\subseteq(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
related to
\delta
(which we denote by
N
) in
[
44
, Equation (6.21)]
is at most

N\leq\left(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})}^{\lceil-\log_{2}(h_{\omega}^{\dagger}(\frac{1}{4}))/4\rceil}\right)^{\Big\lceil\log_{2}(\operatorname{diam}(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}))-\log_{2}\Big(\omega^{\dagger}\big(\frac{\varepsilon_{Q}}{3C_{\eta}C_{\Pi}}\big)\Big)\Big\rceil}.

Second Modification of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
’s proof - Step
5

Following Steps
3
and
4
of the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
verbatim, we can construct an
\omega
-Hölder-like function
47
47

47

Remark that
f^{\star}
is denoted by
f_{N}
in
[
44
]
.
f^{\star}:(\mathbb{R}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})\rightarrow(\Delta_{N},\|\cdot\|_{2})
(defined straightaway after
[
44
, Equation (6.31)]
and
N
is as in (ii) of the statement of Lemma
5.6
) such that its modulus of conitnuity
\omega_{f^{\star}}
satisfies

\omega_{f^{\star}}(t)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}12c^{2}\omega(t)\lceil-\log_{2}(h_{\omega}^{\dagger}(1/4))/4\rceil^{2}\log_{2}(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})})^{2}C_{\eta}/\varepsilon_{A},

and

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\left(f(x),\hat{\eta}\left(f^{\star}(x),Z\right)\right)\leq\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q},

(5.4)

where
Z
is some aptly-chosen matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{N\times D}
(with
N
and
D
as in the statement of Lemma
5.6
). The remaining step in their proof, namely Step
5
, reduces to the approximation of
f^{\star}
using a neural network. It is this step which we now modify (noting that their final Step

remains unaltered in our context); starting from their
[
44
, Equation (6.37)]
.
Let
C_{1}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\max_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:\,\|u\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}=1}\frac{\|u\|_{2}}{\|u\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}}
and
C_{2}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\max_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:\,\|u\|_{2}=1}\frac{\|u\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}}{\|u\|_{2}}
.
Then the
\omega
–Hölder continuous function
f^{\star}:({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})\to(\Delta_{N},\|\cdot\|_{2})
is an
\hat{\omega}
–Hölder continuous function with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
with
\hat{\omega}(t)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}h_{\omega}(C_{2})\omega(t)
.
Now, because
\mathcal{F}_{\cdot}
is a universal Euclidean approximator in the sense of Definition
2.11
, by setting

c^{\varepsilon_{A}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\left\lceil r^{\dagger}(\hat{\omega},K,n,N,\cdot)\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{C_{\eta}\omega\big(\operatorname{diam}(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})\big)\sqrt{N}}\right)\right\rceil

as in
[
44
, (6.37)]
, we deduce the following estimate

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{f}_{n,N}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,N,c^{\varepsilon_{A}}}}\sup_{x\in K}\,\big\|f^{\star}(x)-\hat{f}_{n,N}(x)\big\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle r\left(\hat{\omega},K,n,N,\left\lceil r^{\dagger}(\hat{\omega},K,n,N,\cdot)\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{C_{\eta}\omega\big(\operatorname{diam}(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})\big)\sqrt{N}}\right)\right\rceil\right)

(5.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{C_{\eta}\sqrt{N}\omega\big(C_{1}\operatorname{diam}(K,\|\cdot\|_{2})\big)}.

The remainder of the proof is identical (mutatis mutandis) to the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
;
lastly, we relabel
\varepsilon_{A}
as
\varepsilon_{A}/2
(because we replace
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}}
appearing in the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 3.6]
by
\varepsilon_{A}/2
).

Lastly, we can define
C_{\omega,F_{\varphi},n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}h_{\omega}(C_{2})
(recall
C_{2}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\max_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:\,\|u\|_{2}=1}\frac{\|u\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}}{\|u\|_{2}}
),
C_{\eta,\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\Big(C_{\eta}\omega\big(C_{1}\operatorname{diam}(K,\|\cdot\|_{2})\big)\Big)^{-1}
for
C_{1}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\max_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:\,\|u\|_{F_{\varphi}:N}=1}\frac{\|u\|_{2}}{\|u\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}}
,

C_{\omega}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\lceil-\log_{2}(h_{\omega}^{\dagger}(\frac{1}{4}))/4\rceil
,
C_{\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\omega(\operatorname{diam}(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}))^{-1}
and
C^{\prime}_{\eta,\omega,K,F_{\varphi},n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\Big(6cC_{\eta}\lceil-\log_{2}(h_{\omega}^{\dagger}(1/4))/4\rceil\ln(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})})\Big)^{-1}
.
The conclusion then follows.
∎

We present the proof of the special case of our main result when both
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
admit a a simple global structure. By this, we mean that the singleton
\{(\mathcal{X},\varphi)\}
is a feature decomposition of
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}},\mu)
and
(\mathcal{Y},\eta)
. We obtain this result by applying Lemma
5.6
to approximate the map
f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}:(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\varepsilon_{E}}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n_{\varepsilon_{E}}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
constructed in Lemma
5.5
. Henceforth, for notational simplicity, given a BAP family
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, we will assume that
\operatorname{rank}(T_{n})=n
for all
n
.

Lemma 5.7

(
\mathcal{F}_{\cdot}
-Approximate Extension-Factorization)
.

Let
K
be a non-empty compact doubling subset of a metric space
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})
with doubling constant
C_{(K,d_{\mathcal{X}})}\geq 0
,
(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
be a (barycentric) QAS space,
\mathcal{F}_{\cdot}
be a universal approximator,
\varphi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow F_{\varphi}
be a quasisymmetric feature map such that
\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}
admits a (generalized) Hölder modulus of continuity
\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}
, and
(T_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
realize the
C_{BAP:\varphi(K)}
-BAP on
\varphi(K)
. For every “encoding error”,
\varepsilon_{E}>0
, there is an “encoding dimension”
,n=n_{\varepsilon_{E}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
and an “approximate feature map”,

\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\iota_{T_{n}}^{-1}\circ T_{n}\circ\varphi:(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n}),

(5.6)

with the property that for every
\omega
-Hölder-like map
f:K\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, every “quantization error”
\varepsilon_{Q}>0
and every “approximation error”
\varepsilon_{A}>0
there are
c,N,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, an
\hat{f}_{n,N}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,N,c}
, and
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times D}
such that the following uniform estimate holds

\sup_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{n,N}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\Big)\leq\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{A}.

Furthermore,
n,c,N,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
are bounded above by

(i)

n\leq R^{T_{\cdot}:\varphi(K)}\Big(\omega^{\dagger}_{\varphi^{-1}|_{\varphi(K)}}\circ\omega^{\dagger}\Big(\frac{\varepsilon_{E}}{C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi},T_{\cdot})}}\Big)\Big)
,

(ii)

c\leq\left\lceil r^{\dagger}(C_{\tilde{\omega},F_{\varphi},n}\tilde{\omega},K,n,N,\cdot)\left(C_{\eta,\tilde{\omega},K,F_{\varphi},n}\,\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right\rceil
,

(iii)

D\leq\mathscr{Q}_{f(\mathcal{X})}(\varepsilon_{Q}),

(iv)

\ln(N)\leq C_{\tilde{\omega}}\ln\big(C_{(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})}\big)\Big\lceil-\log_{2}\big(C_{\tilde{\omega},K,F_{\varphi},n}\tilde{\omega}^{\dagger}\big(C^{\prime}_{\eta,\tilde{\omega},K,F_{\varphi},n}\,\varepsilon_{A}\big)\big)\Big\rceil
,

where
\tilde{\omega}(t)=C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi},T_{\cdot})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}(t)
.
Moreover, the above constants
C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi},T_{\cdot})},\,C_{\tilde{\omega},F_{\varphi},n},\ldots,
depend only on the quantities appearing in their respective subscripts.

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

For a fixed
\varepsilon_{E}>0
, let
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}
and
n=n_{\varepsilon_{E}}
be as in Lemma
5.5
. Then, by Lemma
5.5
we have that for every
c,N,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, each
\hat{f}_{n,N}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,N,c}
, and every
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N\times D}
it holds that

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{n,N}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\big)\leq

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x)\big)

(5.7)

\displaystyle+d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{n,N}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{E}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{n,N}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\big).

Next, we claim that
(K,\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})
is doubling.
Since all finite-dimensional Banach spaces are bi-Lipschitz equivalent and bi-Lipschitz maps are quasisymmetric,
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(K)
is a doubling subset of
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})
if and only if it is a doubling subset of
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{2})
. However, all subsets of a Euclidean space are doubling (see, for example,
[
67
, Theorem 12.1]
). Furthermore, as
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}
is continuous and since
K
is compact, we conclude that
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(K)
is a compact doubling subset of
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{\varphi}:n})
; hence, from Lemma
5.6
we deduce that there are
c,N,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, an
\hat{f}_{n,N}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,N,c}
, and a
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N\times D}
(as specified in Lemma
5.6
) such that the following estimate holds

\sup_{u\in\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(K)}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}(u),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{n,N}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\big)\leq\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q}.

(5.8)

Combining (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) yields the desired inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{n,N}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\big)\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{E}+\sup_{u\in\varphi(K)}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}(u),\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}_{N,n}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon_{E}}(x),Z)\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q}.

Lastly, we note that the upper bound for
n=n_{\varepsilon_{E}}
can be found in the statement of Lemma
5.5
, and the upper bounds for
c
,
N
and
D
follow directly from Lemma
5.6
applied to the function
f_{\varphi:\varepsilon_{E}}
whose modulus of continuity is
\tilde{\omega}(t)=C_{(K,\omega,\varphi^{-1},F_{\varphi},T_{\cdot})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi|_{\varphi(K)}^{-1}}(t)
by Lemma
5.5
.
∎

The next lemma shows that the partition of unity which we use in the definition of our approximating class is with high probability well-defined, quantitatively.

Lemma 5.8

(Probabilistic Partition of Unity on a Featurization)
.

Let
\{(\mathcal{X}_{n},\varphi_{n})\}_{n\leq N}
be a feature decomposition of
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}},\mu)
(cf. Definition
3.4
). For every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
48
48

48

Note
n

must be finite
.

n\leq N
and every
0<R\leq\inf_{n\leq N}\,\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{X}_{n})
define the Borel subset
\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}
of
\mathcal{X}
by

\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\,\Big(\mathcal{X}_{i}\setminus[(\partial\mathcal{X}_{i})_{R}\cap\mathcal{X}_{i}]\Big).

(5.9)

For every
\tilde{n}\leq n
the map

\Pi^{(\tilde{n})}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\frac{d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{\tilde{n}}^{c})}{\sum_{i\leq n}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{i}^{c})}

is well-defined.
Moreover, for every
x\in\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}
it holds that
\sum_{\tilde{n}\leq n}\Pi^{(\tilde{n})}(x)=1
.

Proof of Lemma
5.8
.

Fix
1\leq n\leq N
,
0<R\leq\inf_{n\leq N}\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{X}_{n})
, the set
\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}
defined by (
5.9
) is a Borel set.
By construction of
\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}
, for every
x\in\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}
, by definition there is some
i_{x}\leq n
such that
x\in\mathcal{X}_{i_{x}}-[(\partial\mathcal{X}_{i_{x}})_{R}\cap\mathcal{X}_{i_{x}}]
and therefore
d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{i_{x}}^{c})\geq R
; whence,

\sum_{i\leq n}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{i}^{c})\geq d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{i_{x}}^{c})\geq R.

Therefore, for every
1\leq\tilde{n}\leq n
, the map
\Pi^{(\tilde{n})}:\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}\rightarrow[0,1]
is well-defined.
By construction, for every
x\in\mathcal{X}^{(n,R)}
it holds that
\sum_{\tilde{n}\leq n}\Pi^{(\tilde{n})}(x)=1
.
∎

Next, we derive a “deterministic” version of our main result, namely Theorem
3.11
, in the case where
\mathcal{Y}
admits a quantized geodesic partition in the sense of Definition
3.8
. In the lemma below, we will consider a special case of a slightly more general version of the assumptions in our main result, which corresponds to
M=1
, that is, the quantized geodesic partition of
\mathcal{Y}
consists of a singleton
\{\mathcal{Y}\}
. We also allow
\mathcal{Y}
to have a “topologically negligible boundary” as in
[
123
,
124
]
where the boundary can be “deleted” quantitatively. This can be visualized analogously to the illustration in Figure
5
.

Definition 5.9

(
\boldsymbol{L}
-Boundaries)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{L}:(0,1]\rightarrow[0,\infty)
be a decreasing continuous map with
\boldsymbol{L}(1)=0
.
A (possibly empty) subset
\mathcal{Z}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
is called an
\boldsymbol{L}
-boundary if there exists a homotopy
H\in C(\mathcal{Y}\times[0,1],\mathcal{Y})
satisfying:

(i)

H\left(\mathcal{Y},(0,1]\right)\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
,

(ii)

\sup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}(H(y,t),y)\leq\boldsymbol{L}(1-t)
,

(iii)

For each
t\in[0,1)
, the map
y\mapsto H_{t}(y)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}H(y,t)
is Lipschitz.

The next result is a quantitative version of Theorem
3.7
where the target space is a barycentric QAS space after removing an
\boldsymbol{L}
-boundary.

Lemma 5.10

(Transfer Principle: Case
M=1
up to an
\boldsymbol{L}
-Boundary)
.

Let
K
be a non-empty compact subset of a metric measure space
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}},\mu)
with feature decomposition
\{(\mathcal{X}_{n},\varphi_{n})\}_{n\leq N}
and suppose that
\mu
is supported on
K
,

(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}},\hat{\eta})
be a metric space with
\boldsymbol{L}
-boundary
\mathcal{Z}
such that
(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z},d_{\mathcal{Y}},\hat{\eta})
is barycentric QAS space with a contracting barycentric map
\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}
,
let
\mathcal{F}_{\cdot}
be a universal approximator,
and for each
n\leq N
let
\{T_{k}^{(n)}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
realize the BAP on
\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})
.
Suppose that either (or both) of the following hold:

1.

For every
n\leq N
,
\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})
be doubling, and
\varphi_{n}|_{\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}^{-1}
admits a Hölder-like modulus of continuity
\omega_{\varphi_{n}|_{\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}^{-1}}
;

2.

K
is doubling and for each
n\leq N
, the feature map
\varphi_{n}
is quasisymmetric, and
\varphi_{n}|_{\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}^{-1}
admits a Hölder-like modulus of continuity
\omega_{\varphi_{n}|_{\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}^{-1}}
.

For every “encoding error”
\varepsilon_{E}>0
and every
n\leq N
, there is an “encoding dimension”
d_{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

and an “approximate feature map”

\varphi^{(n)}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\iota_{T^{(n)}_{d_{n}}}^{-1}\circ T^{(n)}_{d_{n}}\circ\varphi_{n}:(\mathcal{X}_{n},d_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{n}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{n}:d_{n}}),

(5.10)

with the property that:
for every
\omega
-Hölder-like continuous function
f:K\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, every “quantization error”
\varepsilon_{Q}>0
, every “approximation error”
\varepsilon_{A}>0
and every “confidence level”
\delta\in(0,1]

there exist an integer
N^{\star}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, a finite family
\{(c_{n},D_{n},N_{n})\}_{n\leq N^{\star}}
of triples of positive integers,
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{N^{\star}}
with each
Z_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n}\times D_{n}}
, a family of approximators
\hat{f}_{1},\dots,\hat{f}_{N^{\star}}
with each
\hat{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{d_{n},N_{n},c_{n}}
,
a Borel-function
\hat{T}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
and a Borel
subset
\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
such that:

1.

Deterministic Uniform Approximation:

\sup_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}(\hat{T}(x)),f(x)\big)<\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{A}
,

2.

Randomized Uniform Approximation:

\sup_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)<\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{A},

3.

Representation:

\hat{T}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{n\leq N^{\star}}\,\frac{d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{n}^{c})}{\sum_{i\leq N^{\star}}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{i}^{c})}\,\delta_{\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)},

4.

Probability of Sampling Inputs From
\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
:

\mu(\mathcal{X}_{\delta})\geq 1-\delta
.

Furthermore, for every
n\leq N^{\star}
, the following holds:

(i)

c_{n}\leq\left\lceil r^{\dagger}(\omega_{n},K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},d_{n},N_{n},s_{n})\right\rceil
where
s_{n}=C_{\eta,\omega_{n},K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},F_{n},d_{n}}\,\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{N_{n}^{1/2}}
,

(ii)

d_{n}\leq R^{T_{\cdot}^{(n)}:\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}\Big(\omega^{\dagger}_{\varphi_{n}|_{\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}^{-1}}\circ\omega^{\dagger}\Big(\frac{\varepsilon_{E}}{C_{(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},\omega,\varphi_{n}^{-1},F_{n},T_{\cdot})}}\Big)\Big),

(iii)

D_{n}\leq\mathscr{Q}_{f(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}(\varepsilon_{Q})

,

(iv)

\ln(N_{n})\leq C_{\omega_{n}}\ln\big(C_{(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},\|\cdot\|_{F_{n}:d_{n}})}\big)\Big\lceil-\log_{2}\big(C_{\omega_{n},K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},F_{n},d_{n}}\omega_{n}^{\dagger}\big(C^{\prime}_{\eta,\omega_{n},K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},F_{n},d_{n}}\,\varepsilon_{Q}\big)\big)\Big\rceil
,

(v)

N^{\star}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\inf\big\{N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:\,\mu\big(\mathcal{X}^{(N,1/N)}\big)\,\geq\,1-\delta\big\}
,

(vi)

\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathcal{X}^{(N^{\star},1/N^{\star})},

where
\omega_{n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}C_{(\varepsilon_{A},\omega,K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n},\varphi_{n}^{-1},F_{n},T^{(n)}_{\cdot})}\omega\circ\omega_{\varphi_{n}|_{\varphi_{n}(K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n})}^{-1}}
and
\mathcal{X}^{(N,1/N)}
is defined as in (
5.9
).
In particular, when
N=1
, then
\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathcal{X}
, independently of the choice of
0<\delta\leq 1
; also,
\mu(\mathcal{X}_{\delta})=1
.

Proof of Lemma
5.10
.

Outline:

We prove the result in three steps. First, we restrict each part
\mathcal{X}_{n}
of
\mathcal{X}
, and we perturb
f
’s output to lie in
\mathcal{Y}
minus the
\mathcal{Z}
-boundary. Next, we locally approximate each of these perturbations on each piece
\mathcal{X}_{n}
. Finally, we use a Lipschitz-partition of unity of
\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
to glue each approximation back together, forming our model.

Step 1 - Perturbing
f
on Pieces of
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
’s:

By our hypotheses, there exists an
\boldsymbol{L}
-boundary
\mathcal{Z}
of
\mathcal{Y}
such that
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
is barycentric and admits a quantizable mixing function
\hat{\eta}
. Therefore, there exists a homotopy
H:[0,1]\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
satisfying:
for every
t\in(0,1]

\sup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\left(H_{t}(y),y\right)\leq\boldsymbol{L}(1-t)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad H_{t}(\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}.

(5.11)

For every
\boldsymbol{L}(0)>\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime}>0
set
t^{\star}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}1-\boldsymbol{L}^{\dagger}(\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime})
and note that
t^{\star}
belongs to
(0,1)
. By Definition
5.9
(iii), the map
[y\mapsto H_{t^{\star}}(y)]\in\operatorname{Lip}((\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}}),(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z},d_{\mathcal{Y}}))
is Lipschitz.
For each
n\leq N
define the map

\bar{f}^{(n)}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}H_{t^{\star}}\circ f|_{\mathcal{X}_{n}}:\mathcal{X}_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}.

We note that since
\mathcal{X}_{n}
is closed and since
K
is compact and doubling then for every
n\leq N
the subset
K_{n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n}
of
K
is both compact and doubling
49
49

49

This follows from
[
178
, Lemma 9.3]
together with the observation that the inclusion of
K\cap\mathcal{X}_{n}
into
K
is bi-Lipschitz).
. Because
K_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{n}
,
\bar{f}^{(n)}(K_{n})
is a well-defined subset of
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
. Furthermore, by the definition of
t^{\star}
and the maps
\{\hat{f}^{(n)}\}_{n\leq N}
, and (
5.11
) we have that

\max_{n\leq N}\,\max_{x\in K_{n}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\bar{f}^{(n)}(x)\big)\leq\sup_{x\in f(X)}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(y,H_{t^{\star}}(y)\big)\leq\sup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(y,H_{t^{\star}}(y)\big)\leq\boldsymbol{L}(1-t^{\star})\leq\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime}.

(5.12)

Step 2 - Approximating
f
on Each Part of
\mathcal{X}
:

For every
n\leq N
, suppose that we are given any family of bounded linear operators
\big\{T_{k}^{(n)}\big\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
on
F_{\varphi_{n}}
approximating the identity on
\varphi_{n}(K_{n})
, that each
\varphi_{n}(K_{n})
is a doubling subset of
F_{n}
respectively, and suppose that we are given errors
\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon_{E},\varepsilon_{Q}>0
.
Then, we may apply Lemma
5.7
to the Hölder-like continuous function
\bar{f}^{(n)}
whose modulus of continuity
\bar{\omega}_{n}
only depends on
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{C}
and
\omega
, to conclude that there exist a family
\{(c_{n},d_{n},D_{n},N_{n})\}_{n\leq N}
of
4
-tuples of positive integers (each as in Lemma
5.7
(i)-(iv))
such that for the family of maps
\{\varphi^{(n)}:\mathcal{X}_{n}\rightarrow({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{n}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{n}:d_{n}})\}_{n\leq N}
, defined by

\varphi^{(n)}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\iota_{T_{d_{n}}^{(n)}}^{-1}\circ T_{d_{n}}^{(n)}\circ\varphi_{n}:(\mathcal{X}_{n},d_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{n}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{n}:d_{n}})

,
there exist
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{N}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n}\times D_{n}}
and a family of approximators
\hat{f}_{1},\dots,\hat{f}_{N}\in\mathcal{F}_{\cdot}
, with each
\hat{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{d_{n},N_{n},c_{n}}
, such that the following holds

\sup_{x\in K_{n}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(\bar{f}^{(n)}(x),\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)\Big)<\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime\prime}+\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q},

(5.13)

for each
n=1,\dots,N
.
Together, equation (
5.11
), the definition of
t^{\star}
, and the uniform estimate in (
5.13
), we find that for all
n\leq N

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K_{n}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)\Big)\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K_{n}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),\bar{f}^{(n)}(x)\Big)

(5.14)

\displaystyle+\sup_{x\in K_{n}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(\bar{f}^{(n)}(x),\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime\prime}+\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}.

Setting
\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime\prime}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{2}
and
\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\varepsilon_{C}^{\prime\prime}
we find that (
5.14
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{n\leq N}\,\sup_{x\in K_{n}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\Big(f(x),\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)\Big)\leq\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}.

(5.15)

For notational simplicity, we henceforth abbreviate

\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big).

Step 3 - Gluing the Approximations Together via the Partition of Unity:

We fix
\delta\in(0,1]
. Using the notation of Lemma
5.8
, we define the positive integer
N^{\star}
by

N^{\star}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\inf\big\{1\leq\tilde{N}\leq N:\,\mu\big(\mathcal{X}^{(\tilde{N},1/{\tilde{N}})}\big)\,\geq\,1-\delta\big\}.

Thanks to the properties of Feature Decomposition (cf. Definition
3.4
) we can easily see that
N^{\star}
is always finite for any
\delta>0
.
We set
X_{\delta}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}X^{(N^{\star},1/N^{\star})}
.
By Lemma
5.8
, the family of maps
\{\psi_{n}:\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\rightarrow[0,1]\}_{n\leq N^{\star}}
given by

\psi_{n}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\frac{d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{n}^{c})}{\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\star}}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,X_{i}^{c})}

forms a well-defined locally Lipschitz partition of unity of the metric subspace
(\mathcal{X}_{\delta},d_{\mathcal{X}})
. Moreover, for every
n\leq N^{\star}
,
\inf_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta},\,\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}^{c}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,d(x,\tilde{x})\geq 1/N^{\star}>0
; whence, the
\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
is both closed and open. Therefore, for every
n\leq N^{\star}
and any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
,
\psi_{n}(x)>0
if and only if
x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
. As a consequence, the map
\tilde{T}:\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
defined by

\tilde{T}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)},

(5.16)

is well-defined on
\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
.
Fixing any
y_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}
, we extend
\tilde{T}
to a Borel function
\hat{T}
defined on all of
\mathcal{X}
by

\hat{T}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}I_{\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}(x)\,\tilde{T}(x)+I_{\mathcal{X}_{\delta}^{c}}(x)\,\delta_{y_{0}},

where
I_{\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}
is the measure-theoretic
50
50

50

not in the sense of convex analysis
indicator function. Therefore, the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,W_{1}\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)},\delta_{f(x)}\Big)

(5.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\Big\|\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)}-\delta_{y_{0}}\Big)-\big(\delta_{f(x)}-\delta_{y_{0}}\big)\Big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\Big\|\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)}-\delta_{f(x)}\Big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\Big\|\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)}-\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\delta_{f(x)}\Big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\Big\|\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)}-\delta_{f(x)}\Big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\Big\|\Big(\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)}-\delta_{y_{0}}\Big)-\Big(\delta_{f(x)}-\delta_{y_{0}}\Big)\Big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,W_{1}\Big(\delta_{\hat{f}^{(n)}_{\star}(x)},\delta_{f(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\hat{f}_{\star}^{(n)}(x),f(x)\big)

(5.18)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,(\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{A}.

(5.19)

Since
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
is barycentric, we let
\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}:\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z})\to\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
be the contracting barycentric map with Lipschitz constant
1
such that
\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}(\delta_{y})=y
. Therefore, estimate in (
5.17
)-(
5.19
) implies that

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\hat{T}(x),f(x)\big)=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\hat{T}(x),\beta_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\delta_{f(x)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{x\in K\cap\mathcal{X}_{\delta}}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{E}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{A}.

We note that if
N=1
then we may set
\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathcal{X}
, independently of the choice of
\delta
, and
\psi_{1}
is identically equal to
1
. Finally, the statements on the bounds for
c_{n}
,
d_{n}
,
D_{n}
and
N_{n}
can be obtained by using the corresponding estimates in Lemma
5.7
.
∎

5.2
Proof of Theorem
3.3

We now derive our main
qualitative
universal approximation theorem, which is valid for general metric target spaces and general topological source spaces.
The following is a mild generalization of Theorem
3.3
.
Since the result is qualitative, we opt for a streamlined formulation, in which we aggregate the “quantization error”
\varepsilon_{D}
, “approximation error”, and “encoding error”
\varepsilon_{E}
into a single error
\varepsilon>0
.

Lemma 5.11

(Transfer Principle: Polish
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
)
.

Assume Setting
3.10
(i),(ii), and (iv) and suppose that

(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
is a complete separable metric space, and let
K\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
.
Then, for every continuous
f:(K,d_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}

there is a metric
d_{K}
on
K
generating the subspace topology such that for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists a Lipschitz function
\tilde{f}:(K,d_{K})\to\mathcal{Y}
with
\max_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\tilde{f}(x)\big)<\varepsilon
. Moreover,
for every “error”
\varepsilon>0
and every
n\leq N
, there exist an
N^{\star}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, a family of integers
\{(c_{n},d_{n},N_{n},D_{n})\}_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}
, a family of approximators

\hat{f}_{1},\dots,\hat{f}_{N^{\star}}
with
\hat{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{d_{n},N_{n},c_{n}}
, a Borel set
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
and a Borel map
\hat{T}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
satisfying

\mu\big(\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\big)\geq 1-\max\{\varepsilon,1\},

(5.20)

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}}d_{\mathcal{Y}}(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)})<\varepsilon

and when restricted to
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
, the map
\hat{T}
is of the form

\hat{T}(x)=\sum_{n\leq N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\cdot\,\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}\,[P_{\Delta_{N_{n}}}(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x))]_{i}\,\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{Q_{n}}\,[P_{\Delta_{Q}}(u^{n}_{i})]_{j}\,\delta_{\tilde{y}_{\lceil z_{i,j}^{n}\rceil}}\biggr),

where

(u_{i}^{n},z_{i}^{n})_{i=1}^{N_{n}}
are tensors each in
\mathbb{R}^{2Q_{n}}
for
2Q_{n}=D_{n}
,
\{\tilde{y}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}
is a fixed dense subset in
(\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y}),W_{1})
,
\varphi^{(n)}=\iota_{T^{(n)}_{d_{n}}}^{-1}\circ T^{(n)}_{d_{n}}\circ\varphi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\frac{d_{K}(x,\mathcal{X}_{n}^{c})}{\sum_{j=1}^{N^{\star}}\,d_{K}(x,\mathcal{X}_{j}^{c})}
.

Moreover, if
f
is
\omega
-Hölder-like then we may take
d_{K}=d_{\mathcal{X}}
and if
N=1
then it holds that

\sup_{x\in K}\,W_{1}(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)})<\varepsilon.

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
.
We first introduce some notation.
Let
K\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
,
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}(\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y}),W_{1})
, and denote the
1
-Wasserstein metric on
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})=\mathcal{P}_{1}\big(\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}}),W_{1}\big)
by
\mathcal{W}_{1}
.

Step 1 - Metrizing
\mathcal{X}
so that
f
Can be Approximated by Lipschitz Functions on
K
:

Since
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is continuous when restricted to
\mu
’s support, it belongs to the topological space
f|_{K}\in C(K,\mathcal{Y})
with the uniform convergence on compact sets topology. Since
\mu
is a compactly-supported measure on
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}
is a metrizable Polish space, then
f|_{K}
is a continuous function defined on a compact metrizable Polish space with outputs in a separable metric space
(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
. Hence, Lemma
2.6
applies. Thus, there is a metric
d_{K}
on
K
generating the subspace topology on
K
such that there is a Lipschitz map
\tilde{f}:(K,d_{K})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
satisfying the uniform estimate,

\max_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\tilde{f}(x)\big)<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

(5.21)

Step 2 -
(\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y}),W_{1})
is a Barycentric QAS Space:

First we observe that the map
\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
, given by
y\mapsto\delta_{y}
, is an isometric embedding, whence the map
F:(K,d_{K})\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
, given by
x\mapsto\delta_{\tilde{f}(x)}
, is a Lipschitz map.
We consider the geodesic bicombing
\gamma:\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
sending any
\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}\in\tilde{Y}
and each
t\in[0,1]
to

\gamma(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q},t)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}(1-t)\mathbb{P}+t\mathbb{Q}

and fix some
y_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}
. By the isometric embedding
\Phi
in Lemma
5.1
, for every
t\in[0,1]
and each
\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q},\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
, we have

\displaystyle W_{1}\big(\gamma(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q},t),\gamma(\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{Q}},t)\big)=

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(\gamma(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q},t)-\delta_{y_{0}})-\Phi(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{Q}},t)-\delta_{y_{0}})\big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi\circ\gamma(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q},t)-\Phi\circ\gamma(\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{\mathbb{Q}},t)\big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\|\big((1-t)\Phi(\mathbb{P})+t\Phi(\mathbb{Q})\big)-\big((1-t)\Phi(\tilde{\mathbb{P}})+t\Phi(\tilde{\mathbb{Q}})\big)\big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\|(1-t)(\Phi(\mathbb{P})-\Phi(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}))-t(\Phi(\mathbb{Q})-\Phi(\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}))\big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-t)\big\|\Phi(\mathbb{P})-\Phi(\tilde{\mathbb{P}})\big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}+t\big\|\Phi(\mathbb{Q})-\Phi(\tilde{\mathbb{Q}})\big\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-t)W_{1}(\mathbb{P},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})+tW_{1}(\mathbb{Q},\tilde{\mathbb{Q}})

Hence,
\gamma
is a conical geodesic bicombing on
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
. Since
\mathcal{Y}
is complete and separable then
[
75
, Theorem 6.18]
implies that
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
is also complete. By
[
133
, Theorem 2.6 and Definition 2.4]
, we deduce that
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
admits a
1
-Lipschitz barycenter map
\beta_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}:\mathcal{P}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}})\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
. Also, by the example in Section
2.2
we know that the Wasserstein space
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
is quantizable. Hence, by Proposition
4.6
we conclude that
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
is a barycentric QAS space. From now on, we fix a dense subset
\{\tilde{y}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
and define the mixing function
\hat{\eta}
of the same form as in (
2.6
).

Step 3 - Approximating
F
on Each Part
\mathcal{X}_{n}
:

For each
n\leq N
, we consider the restricted map
F|_{\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}:\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
and note that
\{(\mathcal{X}_{n},\varphi)\}
is a feature decomposition of
(\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K,d_{K})
. Because
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
is a barycentric QAS space, all conditions of Lemma
5.10
are satisfied. Thus, for any
\varepsilon>0
there is a map
\hat{T}_{n}:\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
satisfying

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(\beta_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}\circ\hat{T}_{n}(x),\beta_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}\circ\delta_{F(x)}\big)\leq\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,\mathcal{W}_{1}\big(\hat{T}_{n}(x),\delta_{F(x)}\big)<\varepsilon/2.

(5.22)

In view of Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
5.10
, each
\hat{T}_{n}|_{\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}
is of the form

\hat{T}_{n}|_{\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}=\delta_{\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(\cdot),Z_{n}\big)},

(5.23)

where each
Z_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{n}\times D_{n}}
, while
\hat{f}_{1},\dots,\hat{f}_{N^{\star}}
with each
\hat{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{d_{n},N_{n},c_{n}}
,
\{(c_{n},d_{n},D_{n},N_{n})\}_{n\leq N^{\star}}
is a family of positive integers, and noting that
\varphi^{(n)}
is defined in (
5.10
). Therefore, by the definition of the
1
-Lipschitz barycenter map
\beta_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}:\big(\mathcal{P}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}),\mathcal{W}_{1}\big)\rightarrow(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
and by the definition of the “lifted map”
F
, we have that (
5.22
) implies that

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big),F(x)\big)\leq\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(\beta_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}\circ\hat{T}_{n}(x),\beta_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}\circ\delta_{F(x)}\big)<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}

(5.24)

holds for every
n\leq N
.

Step 4 - Gluing Together the Local Approximators from Step 3:

Step 4 is nearly identical, mutatis mundais, to Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
5.10
. That is, the integer
N^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
are defined in the same way (with
\delta
replaced by
\varepsilon
) and
\tilde{T}:\mathcal{X}_{\delta}\cap K\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
is defined similarly to (
5.16
) by

\tilde{T}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{n\leq N}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)

(5.25)

for a partition of unity
\{\psi_{n}\}_{n=1}^{N^{\star}}
.
As in the previous Lemma’s proof, we take
\hat{T}
to be any Borel extension of
\tilde{T}
from
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\cap K
to all of
\mathcal{X}
.

Step 5 - Explicit Form of
\hat{T}
:

The explicit form of
\hat{T}
follows from the quantized mixing function
\hat{\eta}
defined on
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}
, which was given in (
2.6
) as

\hat{\eta}\big(w,Z_{n}\big)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\hat{\eta}\big(w,(u^{n}_{i,j},z^{n}_{i,j})_{i}^{N_{n},Q_{n}}\big)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}\,[P_{\Delta_{I_{n}}}(w)]_{i}\,\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{Q_{n}}\,[P_{\Delta_{Q_{n}}}(u^{n}_{i,j})]_{j}\,\delta_{\tilde{y}_{\lceil z_{i,j}^{n}\rceil}}\biggr),

where
Z_{n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}(u_{i,j}^{n},z_{i,j}^{n})_{i,j=1}^{N_{n},Q_{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2N_{n}Q_{n}}
,
D_{n}=2Q_{n}
, and
\{\tilde{y}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}
is a specified dense subset in
(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}},d_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}})
.
Together (
5.25
) and (
2.6
) imply that for any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\delta}
, the Borel function
\hat{T}
is of the form

\displaystyle\hat{T}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq N^{\star}}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq N}\,\psi_{n}(x)\,\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}\,[P_{\Delta_{N_{n}}}(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x))]_{i}\,\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{Q_{n}}\,[P_{\Delta_{Q_{n}}}(u^{n}_{i})]_{j}\,\delta_{\tilde{y}_{\lceil z_{i,j}^{n}\rceil}}\biggr)

Combining the estimates in (
5.21
) and (
5.24
) implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big),\delta_{f(x)}\big)\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big),F(x)\big)

\displaystyle+\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(F(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap K}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{\eta}\big(\hat{f}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(n)}(x),Z_{n}\big),F(x)\big)

(5.26)

\displaystyle+\max_{x\in K}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\tilde{f}(x)\big)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\varepsilon.

Finally, if
f
is a (generalized)
\omega
–Hölder function, we can indeed just take
d_{K}=d_{\mathcal{X}}^{\omega}
in this case so that
f:(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}}^{\omega})\to\mathcal{Y}
is a Lipschitz function. If
N=1
, when there is a feature decomposition of
\mathcal{X}
with only one chart, then, as in the proof of Lemma
5.10
, we have
N^{\star}=1
and
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{X}
for any
\varepsilon>0
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

The setting of Theorem
3.3
corresponds to the setup in Lemma
5.11
above with
N=1
and without the assumptions on the doubling space property of
(K,d_{K})
. However, noting that the doubling space assumption is only needed for the quantitative estimates of the upper bounds of parameters
\{c_{n},d_{n},N_{n},D_{n}\}_{n\leq N}
, the “only qualitative” Theorem
3.3
follows immediately from Lemma
5.11
by taking
N=1
and any probability measure
\mu
compactly supported by the compact set
K
.

On the other hand,
we note that the set
\mathcal{K}
of all non-empty finite subsets
\mathcal{X}
is dense in the space of compact subsets of
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})
equipped with the Hausdorff metric; that is,
\mathcal{K}
is typical. Since every finite metric space has the doubling property, for every
K\in\mathcal{K}
,
K
is a doubling subset for the metric
d_{K}
and
\varphi(K)
is a doubling subset of
F
.
Then for any compact subset
\tilde{K}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, any continuous function
f:(\tilde{K},d_{\mathcal{X}})\to(\mathcal{Y},d_{\mathcal{Y}})
and any
\varepsilon>0
, using the denseness of
\mathcal{K}
in the space of compact subsets of
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})
with respect to the Hausdorff metric, one can find a
K_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{K}
with
K_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\tilde{K}
such that
\sup_{x\in\tilde{K}}\inf_{x^{\prime}\in K_{\varepsilon}}d_{\mathcal{Y}}(f(x),f(x^{\prime}))\leq\varepsilon
. Now, applying the quantitative part of Lemma
5.11
to
f|_{K_{\varepsilon}}
, we can find a Borel mapping
\hat{T}_{\varepsilon}:K_{\varepsilon}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
with
\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}W_{1}(\hat{T}_{\varepsilon}(x),\delta_{f(x)})\leq\varepsilon
and obtain quantitative estimates of the parameters
(c,d,N,D)
used in the construction of
\hat{T}_{\varepsilon}
(as now
K_{\varepsilon}
is a doubling set). Thus we also obtain a “quantitative version” of Theorem
3.3
.
∎

5.3
Proof of Theorem
3.11

Proof of Theorem
3.11
.

Denote the support of
\mu
by
K
. We fix an “approximation error”
\varepsilon_{A}>0
, an “encoding error”
\varepsilon_{E}>0
and a quantization error
\varepsilon_{Q}>0
; we let a confidence level
\delta>0
be given.
We define the “modified approximation error”,

{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\leq\min\{\varepsilon_{A},\inf_{n\leq N}\,\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{X}_{n}),1/2\},

which will be specified later. We note that
\varepsilon_{\star:A}>0
can be satisfied because of Definition
3.4
(iv). We fix a positive integer
n\leq N
, which we will choose later.

Step 1 - Removing a Pathological Low-Probability Region in
K
:

Definition
3.8
(i) implies that
\{(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta},\bar{y}_{m})\}_{m\leq M}
is a pairwise disjoint family of subsets of
\mathcal{Y}
whenever
\delta\in[0,1)
. The
\eta
-convexity of
\mathcal{Y}_{m}
implies that for every
\delta\in[0,1]
,
\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}_{m}
. Consequentially,
K\cap f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta}]\subseteq K\cap f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}]
for each
m\leq M
and every
\delta\in[0,1]
. As
{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\in(0,1/2]
, we may define

\delta_{\star}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\max_{m\leq M}\,\sup\Big\{\delta\in[0,1-{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}]:\,\inf_{y\in(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta})_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}}\,d_{\mathcal{Y}}(y,\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{c})\geq\varepsilon_{\star:A}\mbox{ and }(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta})_{\varepsilon_{A:\star}}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}_{m}\Big\}.

We note that the continuity of map
\boldsymbol{S}
in Definition
3.8
implies that
\delta_{\star}
exists. In fact, we can choose
\delta_{\star}=\boldsymbol{S}^{\dagger}(3\varepsilon_{\star:A})
close to
1
for the map
\boldsymbol{S}
.

Upon applying Lemma
5.8
, we have that for every
\tilde{n}\leq n
, the partition of unity map

\Pi^{(\tilde{n})}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\frac{d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{\tilde{n}}^{c})}{\sum_{i\leq n}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{i}^{c})}

is well-defined on
x\in\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}
. We will work on the compact set
K_{\varepsilon_{A}}
defined by

K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigcap\biggl(\bigcup_{m\leq M}f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\bigcap K\biggr),

(5.27)

where the set
\mathcal{X}^{(n,\varepsilon_{\star:A})}
is defined in (
5.9
).
The probability of
K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}
can be approximated from below as follows. Since
\mu
is a probability measure with
K=\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mu\big(K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\big)=

\displaystyle\mu\Big(\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigcap\biggl(\bigcup_{m\leq M}f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\biggr)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\biggl(\mathcal{X}\cap\bigcup_{m\leq M}f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\biggr)-\mu\biggl(\big[\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\big]\bigcap\Big[\bigcup_{m\leq M}\,f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\Big]\biggr)

(5.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\biggl(\bigcup_{m\leq M}f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\biggr)-\mu\biggl(\bigcup_{m\leq M}\,\Big[f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\cap\big[\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\big]\Big]\biggr).

(5.29)

Because
\{f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}]\}_{m\leq M}
is a disjoint family of subsets of
\mathcal{X}
, we can rewrite the right-hand side of (
5.29
) in the form

\displaystyle\mu\big(K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\big)=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\leq M}\,f_{\#}\mu\bigl(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\bigr)-\mu\biggl(\bigcup_{m\leq M}\,\big[f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\cap\big[\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\big]\big]\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{m\leq M}\,f_{\#}\mu\bigl(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\bigr)-\mu\bigl(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\leq M}\,f_{\#}\mu\bigl(\mathcal{Y}_{m}\bigr)-\sum_{m\leq M}\,f_{\#}\mu\bigl(\mathcal{Y}_{m}\setminus\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\bigr)-\mu\bigl(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{m\leq M}\,f_{\#}\mu\bigl(\mathcal{Y}_{m}\setminus\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\bigr)-\mu\bigl(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigr).

(5.30)

Since
g
is geometrically stable (Definition
3.9
) then there are constants
C_{0},q_{1},\dots,q_{M}>0
(depending only on
\mu
,
f
, and on
\{\mathcal{Y}_{m},\bar{y}_{m}\}_{m\leq M}
) such that the right-hand side of (
5.30
) can be re-expressed as

\displaystyle\mu\big(K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\big)\geq

\displaystyle 1-C_{0}\,\sum_{m\leq M}\,(1-\delta_{\star})^{q_{m}}\,f_{\#}\mu(\mathcal{Y}_{m})-\mu\bigl(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigr).

(5.31)

It remains to control the term
\mu\big(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\big)
. Definition (
3.4
) (i)-(iii) imply the following union-bound (that is, subadditivity of measure)

\displaystyle\mu\big(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{(n,{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\big)\leq

\displaystyle\,\mu\biggl(\bigcup_{i>n}\,\mathcal{X}_{i}\biggr)+\sum_{i\leq n}\,\mu\big((\partial\,\mathcal{X}_{i})_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\cap\mathcal{X}_{i}\big)

(5.32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\,n^{-r}+\sum_{i\leq n}\,C_{2}\,\mu\big(\mathcal{X}_{i}\big)\,\varepsilon_{\star:A}^{r_{i}},

(5.33)

where
C_{1},C_{2}>0
are constants depending only on
\mu
and on the feature decomposition
\{(\mathcal{X}_{n},\varphi_{n})\}_{n\leq N}
of the metric measure space
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}},\mu)
.
Incorporating (
5.32
)- (
5.33
) into (
5.34
) implies that

\displaystyle\mu\big(K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\big)\geq

\displaystyle 1-C_{0}\,\sum_{m\leq M}\,(1-\delta_{\star})^{q_{m}}\,f_{\#}\mu(\mathcal{Y}_{m})-C_{1}\,n^{-r}-\sum_{i\leq n}\,C_{2}\,\mu\big(\mathcal{X}_{i}\big)\,\varepsilon_{\star:A}^{r_{i}}.

(5.34)

Clearly, as
\varepsilon_{\star:A}\to 0
and
n\to N
(noting that if
N<\infty
, then for
n=N
the term
C_{1}n^{-r}
will vanish), we have
\delta_{\star}=\boldsymbol{S}^{\dagger}(3\varepsilon_{\star:A})\to 1
and
n^{-r}\to 0
, which ensures that

1-C_{0}\,\sum_{m\leq M}\,(1-\delta_{\star})^{q_{m}}\,f_{\#}\mu(\mathcal{Y}_{m})-C_{1}\,n^{-r}-\sum_{i\leq n}\,C_{2}\,\mu\big(\mathcal{X}_{i}\big)\,\varepsilon_{\star:A}^{r_{i}}\to 1.

Hence, for any given confidence level
\delta\in(0,1)
, we can find a
\varepsilon_{\star:A}\leq\min\{\varepsilon_{A},\inf_{n\leq N}\,\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{X}_{n}),1/2\}
small enough and an
N^{\star}\leq N
large enough such that

\mu\big(K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\big)\geq 1-\delta
for
K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathcal{X}^{(N^{\star},{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})}\bigcap\bigl(\bigcup_{m\leq M}f^{-1}\big[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{{\delta_{\star}}}\big]\bigcap K\bigr)
. From now on let us fix such
\varepsilon_{\star:A}
,
N^{\star}
and
K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}
.

Step 2 - Building the Approximate Partition of Unity,
\hat{C}
, on
f(K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}})
:

We first observe that
\big([0,\infty),|\cdot|\big)
is quantized by the family of maps
\{\mathcal{Q}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}
, where
\mathcal{Q}_{q}:\mathbb{R}^{D_{q}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\mathbb{R}\ni z\mapsto|z|\in[0,\infty)
and

\boldsymbol{\eta}(w,(z_{k})_{k=1}^{N})\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\,w_{k}\,z_{k}
defines a mixing function thereon. Thus,
([0,\infty),|\cdot|)
can be endowed with the structure of a QAS space with quantized mixing function
\hat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
given for any positive integer
k
and any
w\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\times[0,\infty)^{k}
by

\hat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(w,z_{1},\dots,z_{k})\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\,w_{i}\,|z_{i}|.

We recall the notation
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
in Lemma
5.10
for
0<\varepsilon<\inf_{n\leq N}\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{X}_{n})
. Next, we observe that for each
m\leq M
, the map
C_{m}:\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\big([0,\infty),|\cdot|\big)
defined by

C_{m}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(f(x),\mathcal{Y}_{m}\big),

is the composition of a
1
-Lipschitz map (namely
y\mapsto d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(y,\mathcal{Y}_{m}\big)
) with the
\omega
-Hölder-like map,
f
. Hence, each
C_{m}
is itself an
\omega
-Hölder map. Therefore, we may independently apply Lemma
5.10
to functions
C_{1},\ldots,C_{M}
once for each
m=1,\dots,M
, to conclude that there are maps
\hat{C}_{1}
,
\dots,

\hat{C}_{M}:\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\big([0,\infty),|\cdot|\big)
each with representations,

\hat{C}_{m}(x)=\sum_{n\leq N^{\star}}\,\sum_{i\leq\tilde{N}^{(m)}_{n}}\,\frac{d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{n}^{c})}{\sum_{j\leq N^{\star}}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\mathcal{X}_{j}^{c})}\,[P_{\Delta_{\tilde{N}^{(m)}_{n}}}(g_{n}^{(m)}\circ\tilde{\varphi}^{(m)}_{n}(x))]_{i}\,|z^{(m,n)}_{i}|,

(5.35)

where each
g_{n}^{(m)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{d}_{n}^{(m)},\tilde{N}_{n}^{(m)},\tilde{c}_{n}^{(m)}}
,
z_{i}^{(m,n)}\in\mathbb{R}
,
N^{\star}
is defined as in Step 1, and for each
m\leq M
the “approximate feature maps”
\tilde{\varphi}^{(m)}_{n}:\mathcal{X}_{n}\rightarrow(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{d}_{n}^{(m)}},\|\cdot\|_{F_{n}:\tilde{d}_{n}^{(m)}})
are defined by

\tilde{\varphi}^{(m)}_{n}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\iota_{T^{(n)}_{\tilde{d}_{n}^{(m)}}}^{-1}\circ T^{(n)}_{\tilde{d}_{n}^{(m)}}\circ\varphi_{n},

(5.36)

such that, the following uniform estimate holds

\max_{m\leq M}\,\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}}\,\big|C_{m}(x)-\hat{C}_{m}(x)\big|<2^{-3}\varepsilon_{A}.

(5.37)

Because all of mappings
C_{m}(\cdot)
,
m\leq M
are
\omega
–Hölder continuous, the estimates for the family of parameters
(\tilde{c}^{(m)}_{n},\tilde{d}^{(m)}_{n},\tilde{N}^{(m)}_{n})
satisfy the same upper bounds specified in (i), (ii) and (iv) in Lemma
5.10
for all
m\leq M
, where we may set the encoding error
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{E}
, the approximation error
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{A}
and quantization error
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{Q}
for
\hat{C}_{m}
by
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{E}=\tilde{\varepsilon}_{A}=\tilde{\varepsilon}_{Q}=\frac{1}{3}2^{-3}\varepsilon_{A}
.
Since
\{\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}\}_{m\leq M}
forms a disjoint closed cover of
f(\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\cap\bigcup_{m\leq M}\,f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}])
then for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\cap\bigcup_{m\leq M}\,f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}]
there is exactly one integer
1\leq m\leq M
for which
C_{m}(x)
vanishes. Moreover, the estimate in (
5.37
) implies that: if
\hat{C}_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-2}\varepsilon_{A}
then
C_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-1}\varepsilon_{A}
.
However, by the fact that no point in any distinct
\mathcal{Y}_{\tilde{m}}^{\delta_{\star}}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}
at
d_{\mathcal{Y}}
-distance of less than
\varepsilon_{A}/2

(part of the definition of
\delta_{\star}
)
, and note that
\varepsilon_{\star:A}\leq\varepsilon_{A}
, we deduce that for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\cap\cup_{m\leq M}\,f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}]

\hat{C}_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-2}\varepsilon_{A}\Rightarrow C_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-1}\varepsilon_{A}\iff f(x)\in(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}})_{2^{-1}\varepsilon_{A}}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}

(5.38)

holds.
Since the estimate (
5.37
) implies that for every
\tilde{m}\leq M
distinct from
m
we have that
C_{\tilde{m}}(x)>2^{-1}\varepsilon_{A}
, wherefrom we conclude that

\hat{C}_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-2}\varepsilon_{A}\Rightarrow C_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-1}\varepsilon_{A}\iff f(x)\not\in\bigcup_{\underset{\tilde{m}\neq m}{\tilde{m}\leq M}}\,(\mathcal{Y}_{\tilde{m}}^{\delta_{\star}})_{2^{-1}\varepsilon_{A}}.

(5.39)

Together, (
5.38
) and (
5.39
) imply that the map
\hat{C}:\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\big(\Delta_{M},\|\cdot\|_{1}\big)
defined by

\hat{C}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\biggl(\frac{I\big(\hat{C}_{m}(x)\leq 2^{-2}\varepsilon_{A}\big)}{\sum_{\tilde{m}\leq M}\,I\big(\hat{C}_{\tilde{m}}(x)\leq 2^{-2}\varepsilon_{A}\big)}\biggr)_{m\leq M}

is such that
\hat{C}(x)_{m}=1
if and only if
f(x)\in\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}
, whenever
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\cap\cup_{m\leq M}\,f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}]
.
We also note that exactly one component of
\hat{C}(x)
is non-zero, for any
x\in K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
for some suitable
\varepsilon
.

Step 3 - Approximating
f
on Pieces of
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
and Assembling to Approximator via
\hat{C}
:

Since each
\mathcal{Y}_{m}
was
\eta
-convex, then the restriction of the mixing function
\eta
to each
\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\,(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{k}\times\Delta_{k})
is a mixing function on
\mathcal{Y}_{m}
. Therefore, for every positive integer
m\leq M
,
(\mathcal{Y}_{m},d_{\mathcal{Y}}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{m}\times\mathcal{Y}_{m}},\hat{\eta}|_{\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\,(\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{k}\times\Delta_{k})})
is a barycentric QAS space, which by a mild abuse of notation we denote by
(\mathcal{Y}_{m},d_{\mathcal{Y}},\hat{\eta})
. As a consequence, Lemma
5.10
applied to each
\omega
–Hölder continuous function
f|_{K\cap f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}]}
implies that there are maps
\hat{T}^{(m)}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{m})\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
,
m\leq M
, satisfying

\sup_{x\in K\cap f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}]\cap\mathcal{X}^{(N^{\star},\varepsilon_{\star:A})}}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{T}^{(m)}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)<\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{E},

(5.40)

where we have used the fact that each
K\cap f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}]
is compact (because
f
is continuous,
K
is compact, and
\mathcal{Y}_{m}
is closed).
Again in view of Lemma
5.10
, we know that on
\mathcal{X}^{(N^{\star},\varepsilon_{\star:A})}
the mapping
\hat{T}^{(m)}
admits the following representation,

\hat{T}^{(m)}(x)=\sum_{n\leq N^{\star}}\,[C^{\mathcal{X}}(x)]_{n}\,\delta_{f^{(n,m)}(x)},

where
[C^{\mathcal{X}}(x)]_{n}=\frac{d_{\mathcal{X}}(\cdot,\mathcal{X}_{n}^{c})}{\sum_{i\leq N^{\star}}\,d_{\mathcal{X}}(\cdot,\mathcal{X}_{i}^{c})}
,

f^{(n,m)}(x)=\hat{\eta}(\hat{f}^{(m)}_{n}\circ\varphi^{(m)}_{n}(x),Z^{(m)}_{n})
for

\varphi^{(m)}_{n}=\iota_{T^{(n)}_{d_{n}^{(m)}}}^{-1}\circ T^{(n)}_{d_{n}^{(m)}}\circ\varphi_{n}
,

\hat{f}^{(m)}_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{d_{n}^{(m)},N^{(m)}_{n},c^{(m)}_{n}}
,

Z^{(m)}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{(m)}_{n}\times D^{(m)}_{n}}
,
and the family of parameters
(c^{(m)}_{n},d^{m}_{n},N^{(m)}_{n},D^{(m)}_{n})
can be estimated by the upper bounds obtained in Lemma
5.10
(i) – (iv) for all
m\leq M
.

We define the map
\tilde{T}:K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
(noting that
K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\subseteq K\cap\mathcal{X}^{(N^{\star},\varepsilon_{\star:A})}\cap\cup_{m\leq M}f^{-1}[\mathcal{Y}_{m}]
) by

\tilde{T}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\sum_{m\leq M}\,[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,\hat{T}^{(m)}(x).

(5.41)

As in the proof of the previous result, namely Lemma
5.11
, we fix any
y_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}
and extend
\hat{T}
of (
5.41
) to a Borel map
\hat{T}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Y})
by defining

\hat{T}(x)\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\begin{cases}\tilde{T}(x)&:\mbox{if }x\in K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}\\
\delta_{y_{0}}&:\mbox{if }x\not\in K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}.\end{cases}

Step 4 - Verifying the PAC Approximation of
f
by
\hat{T}
:

For every
x\in K_{\varepsilon_{\star:A}}
, the following estimate holds (recalling that
\Phi
denotes the isometric mapping from Lemma
5.1
)

\displaystyle W_{1}\big(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)=

\displaystyle W_{1}\Biggl(\sum_{m\leq M}\,[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,\hat{T}^{(m)}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\Biggr)

(5.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Biggl\|\sum_{m\leq M}[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,\Phi\circ\hat{T}^{(m)}(x)-\Phi(\delta_{f(x))}\Biggr\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Biggl\|\,\sum_{m\leq M}[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,\Phi(\hat{T}^{(m)}(x))-\sum_{m\leq M}\,[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,\Phi(\delta_{f(x)})\Biggr\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Biggl\|\sum_{m\leq M}\,[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,\Phi\Biggl(\hat{T}^{(m)}(x)-\delta_{f(x)}\Biggr)\Biggr\|_{{\text{\AE}}(\mathcal{Y},y_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m\leq M}\,[\hat{C}(x)]_{m}\,W_{1}\big(\hat{T}^{(m)}(x),\delta_{f}(x)\big)

(5.43)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m\leq M}\,I\big(f(x)\in\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}\big)\,W_{1}\big(\hat{T}^{(m)}(x),\delta_{f}(x)\big)

(5.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{E}.

(5.45)

Here, we passed from (
5.43
) to (
5.44
) by using the equivalence relation
\hat{C}(x)_{m}=1
iff
f(x)\in\mathcal{Y}^{\delta_{\star}}_{m}
, and passed from (
5.44
) to (
5.45
) by using the fact that
\{\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}\}_{m\leq M}
is a disjoint cover of
\bigcup_{m\leq M}\,\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}
together with each of the estimates in (
5.40
).

Finally, we set
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon_{\star}:A}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}K_{\varepsilon_{\star}:A}
. By Step 2, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon_{\star}:A}
and each
m=1,\dots,M
, we have that
\hat{C}_{m}(x)=1
if and only of
f(x)\in\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}
. By Step 1,
\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}_{m}
and the sets
\{\mathcal{Y}_{m}^{\delta_{\star}}\}_{m=1}^{M}
are disjoint. Therefore, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon_{\star}:A}
we have that if
\hat{C}_{m}(x)=1
, then the measure
\hat{T}(x)
is supported in
\mathcal{Y}_{m}
. Therefore
\beta_{\mathcal{Y}_{m}}(\hat{T}(x))
is well-defined and since
\beta_{\mathcal{Y}_{m}}
is a contracting barycenter map, the estimate in (
5.44
)- (
5.45
) implies that

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\beta_{\mathcal{Y}_{m}}\circ\hat{T}(x),,\delta_{f(x)}\big)=

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\beta_{\mathcal{Y}_{m}}\circ\hat{T}(x),,\beta_{\mathcal{Y}_{m}}(\delta_{f(x)})\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle W_{1}\big(\hat{T}(x),\delta_{f(x)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{A}+\varepsilon_{Q}+\varepsilon_{E}.

∎

5.4
Proofs for Section
4.3.2

Proof of Lemma
4.18
.

If
q_{j}\in\mathcal{X}
,
j=1,2,\dots
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{X}
then it converges to a limit
q\in H^{k,p}_{0}(M)

as
H^{k,p}_{0}(M)
a Banach space. However, as the
q_{j}
’s are uniformly bounded in
H^{k+1,p}_{0}(M)
,
by the Banach-Alaoglu theorem and the fact that
H^{k+1,p}_{0}(M)
is a reflexive space, we have that

q_{j}
has a subsequence that converges weakly in
H^{k+1,p}_{0}(M)
to a function
q^{\prime}
.
This implies that
q^{\prime}=q
and, hence,
q\in H^{k+1,p}_{0}(M)
. We conclude that
q\in\mathcal{X}
and thus
\mathcal{X}
is a complete metric space.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.19
.

Let
2\leq r\leq k-p/n
be an integer and
f\in H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
.
Let
u_{q,f}(x,t)
be the solution of the wave equation

\displaystyle({\partial}_{t}^{2}-\Delta_{g}+q)u_{q,f}=0\quad\hbox{on }(x,t)\in M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+},

(5.46)

\displaystyle u_{q,f}|_{{\partial}M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}=f(x,t),

(5.47)

\displaystyle u_{q,f}|_{t=0}=0,\quad{\partial}_{t}u_{q,f}|_{t=0}=0.

(5.48)

By Sobolev’s embedding theorem, the identical embedding,
I:H^{k,p}(M)\to C^{r}(\overline{M})

is bounded and hence
q\in C^{r}(\overline{M})
.
By
[
167
, Sec. 2.8]
, see also
[
168
]
, Theorem 2.45 and its proof,

\mathcal{U}_{q}:f\to u_{q,f}
is a bounded linear map

\mathcal{U}_{q}:H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to Z^{r}(M\times[0,T])\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\bigcap_{l=0}^{r}C^{l}([0,T];H^{r-l}(M))\subseteq H^{r}(M\times(0,T)).

Moreover, there is a constant
C_{0}
such that for all integers
s
,
1\leq s\leq r
,

\displaystyle\|u_{q,f}\|_{Z^{s}(M\times[0,T])}\leq C_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))},\quad\hbox{for all }q\in\mathcal{X},

(5.49)

that is, the norm of the map
\mathcal{U}_{q}
is bounded by
C_{0}

for all
q\in\mathcal{X}
.
We emphasize that as
r\geq 2
and
f\in H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
,
the solutions of the initial boundary value problem (
5.46
)-(
5.48
) satisfy
the compatibility conditions

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}^{2j+i}f(x,t)\bigg|_{t=0}=(\Delta_{g}-q)^{j}{\partial}_{t}^{i}u_{q,f}(x,t)\bigg|_{t=0}=0,\quad\hbox{for }x\in{\partial}M

(5.50)

for
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in\{0,1\}
such that
2j+i\leq r-1
.

Also, by
[
167
, Sec. 2.8]
or
[
168
]
, Theorem 2.45,
there is a
c_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{q}\|_{H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to H^{r-1}({\partial}M\times(0,T))}\leq c_{1}

(5.51)

for all
q\in\mathcal{X}
.

We next consider
f\in H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
.
For the functions

q_{1}
and
q_{2}
the difference
w_{q_{1},q_{2},f}=u_{q_{1},f}-u_{q_{2},f}
of solutions satisfies

\displaystyle({\partial}_{t}^{2}-\Delta_{g}+q_{1})w_{q_{1},q_{2},f}=(q_{2}-q_{1})u_{q_{2},f}\quad\hbox{on }(x,t)\in M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+},

\displaystyle w_{q_{1},q_{2},f}|_{{\partial}M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}=0,

\displaystyle w_{q_{1},q_{2},f}|_{t=0}=0,\quad{\partial}_{t}w_{q_{1},q_{2},f}|_{t=0}=0.

As
q_{j}\in\mathcal{X}\subseteq W^{k+1,p}_{0}(M)
, we observe that

\displaystyle F_{q_{1},q_{2},f}=(q_{2}-q_{1})u_{q_{2},f}\in Z^{r}_{0}(M\times[0,T])\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\bigcap_{l=0}^{r}C^{l}([0,T];H^{r-l}_{0}(M)).

(5.52)

By applying again
[
167
, Sec. 2,8]
or
[
168
]
, Theorem 2.45,
we observe that there are constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for all integers
1\leq s\leq r

\displaystyle\|u_{q_{1},f}-u_{q_{2},f}\|_{{Z^{s+1}(M\times[0,T])}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\|F_{q_{1},q_{2},f}\|_{Z^{s}(M\times[0,T])}

(5.53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\|q_{2}-q_{1}\|_{C^{r}(M)}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))}

for all
q_{1},q_{2}\in\mathcal{X}
. By comparing (
5.49
) and (
5.53
), we observe that the map from the boundary value to the difference of the solutions,
f\to u_{q_{1},f}-u_{q_{2},f}
,
is one degree smoother than map from the boundary value to a solution,
f\to u_{q_{j},f}
.

By equation (
5.53
) and the trace theorem in Sobolev spaces
[
166
, Sec. 4, Prop. 4.5]
imply that the normal derivative
{\partial}_{\nu}(u_{q_{1},f}-u_{q_{2},f})|_{{\partial}M\times(0,T)}=(\Lambda_{q_{2}}-\Lambda_{q_{1}})f

satisfies for all integers
1\leq s\leq r
and
f\in H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
,

\displaystyle\|(\Lambda_{q_{2}}-\Lambda_{q_{1}})f\|_{H^{(s+1)-3/2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))}\leq C_{2}\|q_{2}-q_{1}\|_{C^{r}(M)}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))}.

(5.54)

Thus, the above shows that when
r\geq 2
,
k\geq r+p/n
, and
1\leq s\leq r
,
we have that

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{q_{2}}-\Lambda_{q_{1}}\|_{H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to H^{s-1/2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))}\leq C_{2}\|q_{2}-q_{1}\|_{\mathcal{X}}.

(5.55)

Combining (
5.51
) and (
5.55
) we see that the map
H

\displaystyle H:\mathcal{X}\to L(H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T)),H^{r-1}({\partial}M\times(0,T))),\quad q\mapsto\Lambda_{q}

(5.56)

is Lipschitz
when

r\geq 2
and

k\geq r+p/n
.

As
\Lambda_{q}\in L(H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T)),H^{r-1}({\partial}M\times(0,T)))\subseteq L(H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T)),L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T)))
,
the operators

N^{-1/2}:L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to H^{r}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
and

\Lambda_{q}N^{-1/2}:L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
are bounded for
q\in\mathcal{X}
, and
N^{-1/2}
belongs to
HS(L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T)),L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T)))
, the
formula
(
5.56
) implies that the map

\displaystyle\varphi:\mathcal{X}\to F_{\varphi}=HS(L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T)),L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))),\quad q\mapsto(f\mapsto\Lambda_{q}N^{-1}(f)),

(5.57)

is Lipschitz. This proves claim (i).

It remains to show that
\varphi
is injective and prove Hölder-continuity of its left-sided inverse.
As we saw in (
5.54
),
for all
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
1\leq s\leq r

\displaystyle S=S_{q_{1},q_{2}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\Lambda_{q_{1}}-\Lambda_{q_{2}}:H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to H^{s-1/2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))

(5.58)

is bounded and that its norm is bounded by
C_{2}\|q_{2}-q_{1}\|_{\mathcal{X}}
.
To avoid below the difficulties related to interpolation with smoothness indexes
s-\frac{1}{2}
that are in the set
\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}
, we make the trivial observation that by (
5.58
), for all
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
1\leq s\leq r
,

\displaystyle S=S_{q_{1},q_{2}}\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{def.}}}{=}\Lambda_{q_{1}}-\Lambda_{q_{2}}:H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))\to H^{s-1/2-\varepsilon}({\partial}M\times(0,T))

(5.59)

is a bounded operator which norm is bounded by
C_{2}\|q_{2}-q_{1}\|_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Let
X^{\prime}
denote the dual space of a Hilbert space
X
.
When we use the extension of the standard pairing of distributions and test functions to define dual spaces of Sobolev spaces, we have that the dual space of
H_{0}^{s}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
,
s\geq 0
, is
H^{-s}({\partial}M\times(0,T))
, see
[
166
, Sec. 4, Prop. 5.1]
.
Below, we use the shorthand notations
L^{2}({\partial}M\times(0,T))=L^{2}
,
H^{s}({\partial}M\times(0,T))=H^{s}
, and
H^{s}_{0}({\partial}M\times(0,T))=H^{s}_{0}.
By the above,
